'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\\\\nWall time 0.1 seconds\\\\nOutput:\\\\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '56:Temporal synchronization of all data-streams (calcium imaging, visual stimulation, body and eye tracking cameras) was achieved by recording all experimental clocks on a single NI PCI-6612 digital IO board at 100 kHz (Figure 4). All input lines on the synchronization board were sampled simultaneously as a single 32-bit integer at 100 kHz (hardware-timed). To mitigate data size after collection, only changing events and their corresponding sample numbers were stored on a dedicated HDF5 file along with additional metadata.\\\\n58:Two-photon movies (512x512 pixels, 31 Hz for single plane and 512x512 pixels, 11 Hz for each plane in multi-plane experiments), eye tracking (30 Hz), and behavior (30 Hz) were recorded and continuously monitored. Recording sessions were ~1 hour long, but could be interrupted if any of the following was observed: 1) mouse stress as shown by excessive secretion around the eye, nose bulge, and/or abnormal posture; 2) excessive pixel saturation (>1000 pixels) as reported in a continuously updated histogram; 3) loss of baseline intensity caused by bleaching and/or loss of immersion water in excess of 20%; 4) hardware failures causing a loss of data integrity.\\\\n67:5. Animal Stress: Behavior videos were viewed to confirm that animals did not show excessive signs of stress. Any animal that showed eye secretion covering the pupil or excessive orbital tightening was returned to its home cage to recover. The presence of nose bulge, flailing and abnormal postures was also monitored.\\\\n72:10. Interictal Events: Presence of interictal events was assessed by measuring the full field fluorescence and calculating the intensity spike prominence and width for the first 10,000 frames of the 2P movie. Experiments with a non-zero probability of interictal events were then checked manually in order to exclude any potentially epileptic mice (see Steinmetz et al., 2017).\\\\n134:We return normalized, detrended traces of neuronal activity by performing the following algorithm on each trace. First, we estimate the standard deviation of the noise in the trace. We do this by centering the trace on the curve resulting from applying a median filter with kernel size 3.33 seconds to the trace. To avoid including signal events in our estimate of noise, we discard any values that exceed 1.5 times the absolute value of the minimum of this centered trace. We make a first estimate of the standard deviation of noise in the trace as 1.4826 times the median absolute deviation of this truncated, centered trace. We further discard any values that exceed 2.5 times this first estimate of the standard deviation and finally return 1.4826 times the median absolute deviation of the remaining centered trace values as the standard deviation of noise in the trace.\\\\n179:Our dataset contains 8,619 excitatory cells (21 imaging sessions, 9 mice), 470 Sst cells (15 imaging sessions, 6 mice), and 1,239 Vip cells (21 imaging sessions, 9 mice). These cell classes are thought to form a microcircuit whereby Sst and Vip reciprocally inhibit each other (Figure 4B). We performed our analyses on discrete calcium events that were regressed from the raw fluorescence traces, thus removing the slow decay dynamics of the calcium indicator GCaMP6f (Figure 4C). The extracted calcium events are of variable magnitude and correspond to a transient increase in internal calcium levels.\\\\n205:We performed all of our behavioral analysis after assigning behavioral events to each image presentation interval. By image presentation interval we refer to the 750 ms interval beginning with each image presentation. For image omissions we used the 750 ms following the time of the omission, when the image should have been presented. Licks were segmented into licking bouts using an inter-lick interval of 700 ms. This threshold was determined by visual inspection of the histogram of inter-lick intervals. The start and end of each licking bout was then assigned to an image presentation interval.\\\\n208:For all analysis of neural data we used the detected calcium events as described in Garrett et al.41 and https://portal.brain-map.org/explore/circuits/visual-behavior-2p. This process produces, for each cell, a set of calcium events each with a time and magnitude. Except where noted we used familiar image set sessions collected on the multiplane calcium imaging rig.\\\\ncode/allensdk/brain_observatory/behavior/eye_tracking_processing.py:9:class EyeTrackingError(Exception):\\\\ncode/allensdk/brain_observatory/behavior/eye_tracking_processing.py:66:        desired.    0B          0B          0B\\\\n            loop over `self[session]`, so results do not depend on the thread count.\\\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\\\n        if workers is None:\\\\n            workers pending.popleft().result()\\\\n\\\\n    def __getitem__(self, session: int):\\\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Build one session\\\\'s tensors from its per-trial arrays.\\\\n\\\\n        Args:\\\\n            session: index into the per-session lists.\\\\n\\\\n        Returns:\\\\n            {\\\\'neural\\\\': float32 (rows, nneurons), \\\\'input\\\\': float32 (rows, dinput),\\\\n             \\\\'output\\\\': int64 (rows, doutput), \\\\'nneurons\\\\': int}, where rows is the\\\\n            session\\\\'s total timepoints; or None when the session has no trials,\\\\n            which is what the previous version stored for it.\\\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\\\n        trials = self._neural[session]\\\\n        if len(trials) == 0:\\\\n            return None\\\\n\\\\n        rows = sum(trial.shape[1] for trial in trials)\\\\n        nneurons = trials[0].shape[0]\\\\n\\\\n        # Preallocate and fill, rather than\\\\n        # np.concatenate([trial.T.astype(np.float32) for trial in trials]): that\\\\n        # holds a converted copy of every trial AND the concatenated result at the\\\\n        # same moment. Assigning into the destination casts in place, so neither\\\\n        # temporary is ever allocated. Per-trial inputs and outputs are broadcast\\\\n        # into their row block instead of being np.tile\\\\'d first -- tile built a\\\\n        # (d, T) array per trial only to transpose and copy it again.\\\\n        neural_concat = np.empty((rows, nneurons), np.float32)\\\\n        input_concat = np.empty((rows, self.dinput), np.float32)\\\\n        output_concat = np.empty((rows, self.doutput), np.int64)\\\\n\\\\n        row = 0\\\\n        for trial in range(len(trials)):\\\\n            T = trials[trial].shape[1]\\\\n            neural_concat[row:row + T] = trials[trial].T\\\\n            input_trial = self._input[session][trial]\\\\n            output_trial = self._output[session][trial]\\\\n            # A 1-D per-trial value broadcasts across the block\\\\'s T rows.\\\\n            input_concat[row:row + T] = (input_trial.T if input_trial.ndim == 2\\\\n                                         else input_trial)\\\\n            output_concat[row:row + T] = (output_trial.T if output_trial.ndim == 2\\\\n                                          else output_trial)\\\\n            row += T\\\\n\\\\n        # For discrete inputs, use a one-hot encoding.\\\\n        output: variables we want to decode. output is a list of length nsessions, output[session] is a list of length ntrials[session],\\\\n                and output[session][trial] is a numpy array of shape (doutput, T[session][trial]) of dtype = int with\\\\n                categorical values for each timepoint. Each output[idx, t] is an integer from 0 to ncategories[idx]-1.\\\\n        metadata: optional dict with additional information about the experiment.\\\\n\\\\n        Algorithm hyperparameters can be passed as keyword arguments:\\\\n        npcs: number of projected dimensions to use (default: 10)\\\\n        num_epochs: number of training epochs (default: 200)\\\\n        lr: learning rate (default: 0.001)\\\\n        l1_weight: L1 regularization weight for projection matrices (default: 1e-4)\\\\n        svd_max_samples: maximum number of timepoints to use for SVD initialization (default: 50000).\\\\n                         If a session has more timepoints than this, a random subset will be used.\\\\n        svd_max_neurons: maximum number of neurons to use for SVD initialization (default: 2000).\\\\n                         If a session has more neurons than this, random Gaussian projection will be used.\\\\n                         The final weight is the product of the random projection and SVD matrices.\\\\n        ncategories: optional list of length doutput giving number of categories for each output dimension.\\\\n                     If not provided, will be inferred from data.\\\\n        balanced_loss: if True, use class-weighted cross-entropy loss to compensate for class imbalance.\\\\n                       Weights are inverse of class frequency. Default: False.\\\\n\\\\n    Returns:\\\\n        model: dict with trained decoder parameters. dict with the keys:\\\\n            \\\\'projection\\\\': list of length nsessions, projection[session] is a torch tensor of shape (npcs, nneurons[session])\\\\n            \\\\'decoder\\\\': list of length doutput, decoder[out_dim] is a torch linear layer for that output dimension\\\\n            \\\\'ncategories\\\\': list of length doutput, ncategories[out_dim] is the number of categories for that output\\\\n            \\\\'device\\\\': torch device used for training\\\\n\\\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\\\n\\\\n    model = {\\\\n        \\\\'projection\\\\': [],\\\\n        \\\\'decoder\\\\': [],\\\\n        \\\\'ncategories\\\\': [],\\\\n        \\\\'device\\\\': None\\\\n    }\\\\n\\\\n    npcs = kwargs.get(\\\\'npcs\\\\', 10)\\\\n    num_epochs = kwargs.get(\\\\'num_epochs\\\\', 200)\\\\n    lr = kwargs.get(\\\\'lr\\\\', 0.001)\\\\n    l1_weight = kwargs.get(\\\\'l1_weight\\\\', 1e-4)\\\\n    device_str = kwargs.get(\\\\'device\\\\', None)\\\\n    balanced_loss = kwargs.get(\\\\'balanced_loss\\\\', False)\\\\n\\\\n    nsessions = len(neural)\\\\n    if device_str is not None:\\\\n        device = torch.device(device_str)\\\\n    else:\\\\n        device = torch.device(\\\\'cuda\\\\' if torch.cuda.is_available() else \\\\'cpu\\\\')\\\\n\\\\n    model[\\\\'device\\\\'] = device\\\\n    print(\\\\'Using device:\\\\', device)\\\\n\\\\n    # Prepare training data using helper function\\\\n    session_data, dinput, doutput = _prepare_session_data(neural, input, output, device)\\\\n\\\\n    # Determine number of categories for each output dimension\\\\n    if \\\\'ncategories\\\\' in kwargs:\\\\n        ncategories = kwargs[\\\\'ncategories\\\\']\\\\n    else:\\\\n        # Session is the OUTER loop so each one is fetched once for all output\\\\n        # dimensions. With session inner, session_data[session] would rebuild the\\\\n        # same session\\\\'s tensors doutput times. The result does not depend on the\\\\n        # order.\\\\n        max_cat = [0] * doutput\\\\n        for session in range(nsessions):\\\\n            batch = session_data[session]\\\\n            if batch is None:\\\\n                continue\\\\n            for out_dim in range(doutput):\\\\n                max_cat[out_dim] = max(max_cat[out_dim],\\\\n                                       int(batch[\\\\'output\\\\'][:, out_dim].max().item()))\\\\n        ncategories = [m + 1 for m in max_cat]\\\\n\\\\n    model[\\\\'ncategories\\\\'] = ncategories\\\\n\\\\n    # Compute class weights (always, for use in chance computation and optionally for balanced loss)\\\\n    class_weights = []\\\\n    class_fractions = []  # Store fractions for chance computation\\\\n    # Session outer, output dimension inner, for the same reason as ncategories\\\\n    # above: one fetch per session instead of doutput of them. Summation order\\\\n    # within a class count is unchanged.\\\\n    class_counts_all = [np.zeros(ncategories[out_dim]) for out_dim in range(doutput)]\\\\n    for session in range(nsessions):\\\\n        batch = session_data[session]\\\\n        if batch is None:\\\\n            continue\\\\n        for out_dim in range(doutput):\\\\n            outputs = batch[\\\\'output\\\\'][:, out_dim].cpu().numpy()\\\\n            for c in range(ncategories[out_dim]):\\\\n                class_counts_all[out_dim][c] += np.sum(outputs == c)\\\\n\\\\n    for out_dim in range(doutput):\\\\n        class_counts = class_counts_all[out_dim]\\\\n        # Store class fractions for chance computation\\\\n        total = np.sum(class_counts)\\\\n        fractions = class_counts / total\\\\n        class_fractions.append(fractions)\\\\n        # Compute weights as inverse of frequency (with smoothing to avoid division by zero)\\\\n        weights = total / (ncategories[out_dim] * np.maximum(class_counts, 1))\\\\n        # Normalize weights to sum to 1 (so each output dimension is equally weighted)\\\\n        weights = weights / np.sum(weights)\\\\n        class_weights.append(torch.tensor(weights, dtype=torch.float32, device=device))\\\\n\\\\n    model[\\\\'class_weights\\\\'] = class_weights\\\\n    model[\\\\'class_fractions\\\\'] = class_fractions\\\\n\\\\n    if balanced_loss:\\\\n        print(f\\\"Using balanced loss with class weights:\\\")\\\\n        for out_dim in range(doutput):\\\\n            print(f\\\"  Output {out_dim}: {class_weights[out_dim].cpu().numpy()}\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    # below would otherwise rebuild this session\\\\'s tensors four times.\\\\n        batch = session_data[session]\\\\n        if batch is None:\\\\n            # A session with no trials has no neuron count to size a layer with. Held as\\\\n            # None so that projection_layers stays indexed by session; every read of it is\\\\n            # guarded by the same emptiness check that skips the session\\\\'s data.\\\\n            projection_layers.append(None)\\\\n            continue\\\\n        nneurons = batch[\\\\'nneurons\\\\']\\\\n        proj = nn.Linear(nneurons, npcs, bias=False).to(device)\\\\n\\\\n        # Initialize with SVD for stability (use V from SVD of neural data)\\\\n        # For neural data with shape (n_timepoints, n_neurons), SVD gives principal components in V\\\\n        # Use random subsets if data is too large\\\\n        n_timepoints = batch[\\\\'neural\\\\'].shape[0]\\\\n\\\\n        # Use random projection if too many neurons\\\\n        if nneurons > svd_max_neurons:\\\\n            # Create random Gaussian projection matrix: (n_neurons, svd_max_neurons)\\\\n            P = torch.randn(nneurons, svd_max_neurons)\\\\n            # Project neural data: (n_timepoints, n_neurons) @ (n_neurons, svd_max_neurons) = (n_timepoints, svd_max_neurons)\\\\n            neural_projected = neural_subset @ P\\\\n            print(f\\\"Session {session}: Using random projection to {svd_max_neurons} of {nneurons} neurons for SVD initialization\\\")\\\\n\\\\n            # SVD on projected data\\\\n            _, _, V = torch.svd(neural_projected, some=True)\\\\n            # V has shape (svd_max_neurons, min(n_timepoints_subset, svd_max_neurons))\\\\n\\\\n            # Compose: final weight = (P @ V[:, :npcs]).T = V[:, :npcs].T @ P.T\\\\n            n_components = min(npcs, V.shape[1])\\\\n            proj.weight.data[:n_components, :] = (V[:, :n_components].T @ P.T).to(device).clone()\\\\n        else:\\\\n            # Standard SVD without random projection\\\\n            _, _, V = torch.svd(neural_subset, some=True)\\\\n            # V has shape (n_neurons, min(n_timepoints_subset, n_neurons))\\\\n            n_components = min(npcs, V.shape[1])\\\\n            proj.weight.data[:n_components, :] = V[:, :n_components].T.to(device).clone()\\\\n\\\\n        # File: /app/convert_data.py\\\\n+#!/usr/bin/env python3\\\\n+\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Convert the supplied Allen Visual Behavior 2P NWBs for neural decoding.\\\\n+\\\\n+The conversion follows the set.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    matches = [\\\\n+        group for group in nwb[\\\\\\\"intervals\\\\\\\"].values()\\\\n+        if isinstance(group, h5py.Group)\\\\n+        and \\\\\\\"image_name\\\\\\\" in group\\\\n+        and \\\\\\\"omitted\\\\\\\" in group\\\\n+        and \\\\\\\"trials_id\\\\\\\" in group\\\\n+    ]\\\\n+    if len(matches) != 1:\\\\n+        raise RuntimeError(\\\\n+            f\\\\\\\"Expected one natural-image presentation table, found {len(matches)}\\\\\\\"\\\\n+        )\\\\n+    return matches[0]\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _eligible_trial_ids(nwb: h5py.File) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\\\\n+    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Get requested Go/Catch trial IDs and their four-way outcomes.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    trials = nwb[\\\\\\\"intervals/trials\\\\\\\"]\\\\n+    keep = (\\\\n+        (trials[\\\\\\\"go\\\\\\\"][:] | trials[\\\\\\\"catch\\\\\\\"][:])\\\\n+        & presentations[\\\\\\\"is_change\\\\\\\"][:].astype(bool)\\\\n+\\\\n+    row_groups: list[np.ndarray] = []\\\\n+    starts: list[np.ndarray] = []\\\\n+    names: list[np.ndarray] = []\\\\n+    changes: raise RuntimeError(f\\\\\\\"Retained trial {trial_id} has no active presentations\\\\\\\")\\\\n+        # The NWB table is chronological, but make the assumption explicit.\\\\n+        idx = starts.append(starts_source[idx])\\\\n+        names.append(names_source[idx])\\\\n+        changes.append(changes_source[idx])\\\\n+\\\\n+    return (\\\\n+ _interval_sums(\\\\n+    timestamps: np.ndarray, values: h5py.Dataset, starts: np.ndarray\\\\n+) -> np.ndarray:\\\\n+    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Sum time x cell event magnitudes in fixed presentation intervals.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    first = int(np.searchsorted(timestamps, starts[0], side=\\\\\\\"left\\\\\\\"))\\\\n+    last = int(np.searchsorted(timestamps, starts[-1] + BIN_SECONDS, \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Average a finite behavioral trace over presentation intervals.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    prefix = np.empty(len(values_at_reference) + 1, dtype=np.float64)\\\\n+        reference_timestamps, starts + BIN_SECONDS, side=\\\\\\\"left\\\\\\\"\\\\n+    )\\\\n+    counts = right - left\\\\n+    if np.any(counts == 0):\\\\n+        raise RuntimeError(\\\\\\\"A presentation interval contains no ophys timestamps\\\\\\\")\\\\n+    return (prefix[right] - prefix[left]) / counts\\\\n+\\\\n+\\\\n+def _interpolate_finite(\\\\n+    source_timestamps:         continue\\\\n+            trial_ids, _ = _eligible_trial_ids(nwb)\\\\n+            if len(trial_ids) < 2:\\\\n+                excluded.append({\\\\n+ For each eligible trial ID (from `intervals/trials`), active stimulus presentations carrying that trial's `trials_id` are collected. Each trial becomes a variable-length sequence of 750 ms presentation intervals (typically 10-17 presentations per trial). This differs from the reference approach which uses the full trial window from `start_time` to `stop_time`.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef _eligible_trial_ids(nwb):\n    trials = nwb[\"intervals/trials\"]\n    keep = (\n        (trials[\"go\"][:] | trials[\"catch\"][:])\n        & ~trials[\"aborted\"][:]\n        & ~trials[\"auto_rewarded\"][:]\n    )\n    trial_ids = trials[\"id\"][:][keep].astype(np.int64)\n    ...\n\ndef _session_presentations(nwb, trial_ids):\n    presentations = _presentation_group(nwb)\n    source_trial_ids = presentations[\"trials_id\"][:]\n    active = presentations[\"active\"][:].astype(bool)\n    ...\n    for trial_id in trial_ids:\n        idx = np.flatnonzero((source_trial_ids == trial_id) & active)\n        ...\n```\n\niii. The agent chose this approach because: \"The reference paper's actual analysis unit is the 750 ms image-presentation interval (250 ms image plus 500 ms gray), and omissions are treated as their own 750 ms intervals.\"\n\n## 1-e. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Event detection magnitudes are **summed** within each 750 ms presentation interval using a prefix-sum approach. Planes from the same session are concatenated along the neuron axis.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef _interval_sums(timestamps, values, starts):\n    first = int(np.searchsorted(timestamps, starts[0], side=\"left\"))\n    last = int(np.searchsorted(timestamps, starts[-1] + BIN_SECONDS, side=\"left\"))\n    event_data = np.empty((last - first, values.shape[1]), dtype=np.float32)\n    values.read_direct(event_data, source_sel=np.s_[first:last, :])\n    prefix = np.empty((len(event_data) + 1, event_data.shape[1]), dtype=np.float32)\n    prefix[0] = 0.0\n    np.cumsum(event_data, axis=0, dtype=np.float32, out=prefix[1:])\n    left = np.searchsorted(timestamps, starts, side=\"left\") - first\n    right = np.searchsorted(timestamps, starts + BIN_SECONDS, side=\"left\") - first\n    return prefix[right] - prefix[left]\n```\n\niii.
